\displaystyle\begin{cases}\bm{x}_{1}=x_{1}\\
\bm{x}_{2}=x_{2}\end{cases}\qquad\text{ and}\qquad\quad\begin{cases}\bm{x}=x_{1}\\
\bm{y}=x_{2}-x_{1}.\end{cases}

The first series (
6.1
) will correspond to an
expansion in the regime
0<x_{1}\ll x_{2}
,
and the second (
6.2
)
to and expansion in the regime
0<x_{2}-x_{1}\ll x_{1}
.
As discussed in Section
5
, the
series (
6.1
) is in the space

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[\bm{x}_{2}]\,[[\bm{x}_{1}/\bm{x}_{2}]]\,[\bm{x}_{1}^{-1}]\;\bm{x}_{1}^{{\Delta}_{1}}\,\bm{x}_{2}^{{\Delta}_{2}}\qquad\text{ with }\qquad\begin{cases}{\Delta}_{1}=h({\varsigma})-h(\lambda_{1})-h(\lambda_{0})\\
{\Delta}_{2}=h(\lambda_{\infty})-h(\lambda_{2})-h({\varsigma}).\end{cases}

Similarly we will see that the
series (
6.2
) is in the space

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[\bm{x}]\,[[\bm{y}/\bm{x}]]\,[\bm{y}^{-1}]\;\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}\,\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})}\qquad\text{with }\qquad\begin{cases}\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})=h({\mu})-h(\lambda_{2})-h(\lambda_{1})\\
\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})=h(\lambda_{\infty})-h({\mu})-h(\lambda_{0}).\end{cases}

The goal of this section is to prove the following
associativity of intertwining operators.

Theorem 6.1
.

Fix
\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{\infty}\in\mathbb{N}
.
For arbitrary

w_{0}\in Q_{\lambda_{0}}
,

w_{1}\in Q_{\lambda_{1}}
,

w_{2}\in Q_{\lambda_{2}}
,

w_{\infty}^{\prime}\in Q_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime}
,
and
{\varsigma}\in\mathrm{Sel}(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})
,
the series

\displaystyle\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,{\varsigma}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}(w_{2},x_{2})\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{1}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;{\varsigma}}(w_{1},x_{1})\,w_{0}},

converges when
0<x_{1}<x_{2}
, and the series

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mu}\,\in\,\mathrm{Sel}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\,\cap\,\mathrm{Sel}(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{\infty})}\Big\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\lambda_{2}}&{\lambda_{1}}&{{\mu}}\\
{\lambda_{0}}&{\lambda_{\infty}}&{{\varsigma}}\end{array}\Big\}\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},x_{2}-x_{1})\,w_{1},\,x_{1}\big)\,w_{0}}

converges when
0<x_{2}-x_{1}<x_{1}
, and in the (nontrivial) overlap
of these domains, the analytic functions represented by these two series coincide.

The proof of Theorem
6.1
consists of two main steps:
the special case of the matrix element of highest weight vectors,
and the recursion on the PBW filtration to establish the general
case. We address the two in separate subsections below.

In the rest of the section, sums
over
{\mu}
are always taken over the same set of values satisfying the two
selection rules above.
For brevity, we omit this from the notation and write just

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mu}}\Big(\cdots\Big)\;:=\;\sum_{{\mu}\,\in\,\mathrm{Sel}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\,\cap\,\mathrm{Sel}(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{\infty})}\Big(\cdots\Big).

6.2.
The special case with highest weight vectors

The first step in the proof of Theorem
6.1
is
to consider the special case where instead of general vectors

w_{0}\in Q_{\lambda_{0}}
,

w_{1}\in Q_{\lambda_{1}}
,

w_{2}\in Q_{\lambda_{2}}
,

w_{\infty}^{\prime}\in Q_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime}
,
we use the highest weight vectors

\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}\in Q_{\lambda_{0}}
,

\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}}\in Q_{\lambda_{1}}
,

\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}}\in Q_{\lambda_{2}}
,

\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime}\in Q_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime}
.
This is analogous to the initial term of an intertwining
operator, and the highest weight matrix element considered
in the context of compositions of intertwining operators.
The precise statement we want to prove is the following.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{\infty}\in\mathbb{N}
.
For arbitrary
{\varsigma}\in\mathrm{Sel}(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1})
, the two series

\displaystyle\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,{\varsigma}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}},x_{2})\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{1}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;{\varsigma}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},x_{1})\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}},

converges when
0<x_{1}<x_{2}
, and the series

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mu}}\Big\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\lambda_{2}}&{\lambda_{1}}&{{\mu}}\\
{\lambda_{0}}&{\lambda_{\infty}}&{{\varsigma}}\end{array}\Big\}\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}},x_{2}-x_{1})\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},\,x_{1}\big)\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}}

converges when
0<x_{2}-x_{1}<x_{1}
, and in the (nontrivial) overlap
of these domains, the analytic functions represented by these two series coincide.

The first of the two series is exactly of the form considered in
Section
5
, and by
Theorem
5.9

we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u_{\underline{{\varsigma}}}](0,x_{1},x_{2})\;=\;\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,{\varsigma}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}},x_{2})\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{1}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;{\varsigma}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},x_{1})\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}}\qquad\text{for $0<x_{1}<x_{2}$,}

where
u_{\underline{{\varsigma}}}\in\mathsf{M}_{\lambda_{2}}\otimes\mathsf{M}_{\lambda_{1}}\otimes\mathsf{M}_{\lambda_{0}}

is the conformal block vector (
3.5
)

\displaystyle u_{\underline{{\varsigma}}}\;=\;\Big(\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathsf{M}_{\lambda_{2}}}\otimes{\iota}_{\;{{\varsigma}}}^{{\lambda_{1}},{\lambda_{0}}}\big)\circ{\iota}_{\;{\lambda_{\infty}}}^{{\lambda_{2}},{{\varsigma}}}\Big)\,(u_{0}^{(\lambda_{\infty})}).

For the purposes of comparing with the other series in the associativity
statement, we decompose this vector using the defining
property (
3.1
)

\displaystyle\Big(\big(\mathrm{id}_{\mathsf{M}_{\lambda_{2}}}\otimes{\iota}_{\;{{\varsigma}}}^{{\lambda_{1}},{\lambda_{0}}}\big)\circ{\iota}_{\;{\lambda_{\infty}}}^{{\lambda_{2}},{{\varsigma}}}\Big)=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mu}}\Big\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\lambda_{2}}&{\lambda_{1}}&{{\mu}}\\
{\lambda_{0}}&{\lambda_{\infty}}&{{\varsigma}}\end{array}\Big\}\big({\iota}_{\;{{\mu}}}^{{\lambda_{2}},{\lambda_{1}}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathsf{M}_{\lambda_{0}}}\big)\circ{\iota}_{\;{\lambda_{\infty}}}^{{{\mu}},{\lambda_{0}}}

of
6j
-symbols, to get

\displaystyle u_{\underline{{\varsigma}}}\;=\;\sum_{{\mu}}\Big\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\lambda_{2}}&{\lambda_{1}}&{{\mu}}\\
{\lambda_{0}}&{\lambda_{\infty}}&{{\varsigma}}\end{array}\Big\}\;\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}},\qquad\text{ where}\qquad\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}}\;=\;\Big(\big({\iota}_{\;{{\mu}}}^{{\lambda_{2}},{\lambda_{1}}}\otimes\mathrm{id}_{\mathsf{M}_{\lambda_{0}}}\big)\circ{\iota}_{\;{\lambda_{\infty}}}^{{{\mu}},{\lambda_{0}}}\Big)\,(u_{0}^{(\lambda_{\infty})}).

For the corresponding function, we get the decomposition

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u_{\underline{{\varsigma}}}](0,x_{1},x_{2})\;=\;\sum_{{\mu}}\Big\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\lambda_{2}}&{\lambda_{1}}&{{\mu}}\\
{\lambda_{0}}&{\lambda_{\infty}}&{{\varsigma}}\end{array}\Big\}\;\mathcal{F}[\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}}](0,x_{1},x_{2}),

where each term on the right hand side has a good Frobenius series
expansion in the variable
x_{2}-x_{1}
, by virtue of the submodule
projection property
\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}}={\pi}_{\{{1},{2}\}}^{\;\;{{\mu}}}(\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}})

and Lemma
2.5
.
Specifically, for any
{\mu}
, in the region
0<x_{2}-x_{1}<x_{1}
, we have

(6.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}}](0,x_{1},x_{2})\;=\;(x_{2}-x_{1})^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})}\,\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\widehat{C}_{k}(x_{1})\,(x_{2}-x_{1})^{k},

where
\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})=h({\mu})-h(\lambda_{2})-h(\lambda_{1})

and the leading coefficient is

\displaystyle\widehat{C}_{0}(x_{1})\;=\;\;

\displaystyle B_{{\lambda_{2}},{\lambda_{1}}}^{\;{\;\;{\mu}}}\;\mathcal{F}\big[{\iota}_{\;{\lambda_{\infty}}}^{{{\mu}},{\lambda_{0}}}(u_{0}^{(\lambda_{\infty})})\big](0,x_{1})\;=\;B_{{\lambda_{2}},{\lambda_{1}}}^{\;{\;\;{\mu}}}\,B_{{{\mu}},{\lambda_{0}}}^{\;{\,\lambda_{\infty}}}\;x_{1}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}

with

\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})=h(\lambda_{\infty})-h({\mu})-h(\lambda_{0})
.
To prove Proposition
6.2

therefore amounts to showing that the
series (
6.3
)
coincides with the other expression with VOA intertwining operators,

\displaystyle\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}},\bm{y})\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},\,\bm{x}\big)\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}},

where one substitutes
\bm{x}=x_{1}
and
\bm{y}=x_{2}-x_{1}
.
For this, the strategy is again to show that both expressions satisfy
the same differential equation, and that the series solutions to it are unique
up to multiplicative constants.
The most straightforward approach would be to use more than one differential
equation (e.g., inductively, as in Section
5
),
but in the current case we can in fact bypass the need for all but one
differential equation by using a priori homogeneity information.

The appropriate differential operator now is

(6.4)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}=\sum_{k=1}^{\lambda_{2}+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\dots,p_{k}\geq 1\\
p_{1}+\cdots+p_{k}=\lambda_{2}+1\end{subarray}}\frac{(-4/\kappa)^{1+\lambda_{2}-k}\lambda_{2}!}{\prod_{u=1}^{k-1}(\sum_{i=1}^{u}p_{i})(\sum_{i=u+1}^{k}p_{i})}\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{-p_{1}}^{(2)}\cdots\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{-p_{k}}^{(2)}

where for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
we set

(6.5)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{n}^{(2)}:=\;

\displaystyle-(-x-y)^{n}\left((-x-y)\frac{\partial}{\partial{x}}+(1+n)h(\lambda_{0})\right)

\displaystyle-(-y)^{n}\left((-y)\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial{x}}-\frac{\partial}{\partial{y}}\Big)+(1+n)h(\lambda_{1})\right).

Analogously to Section
5
,
these differential operators act on the one hand on
spaces of smooth functions of
x
and
y
, and on the other hand on
spaces

\mathbb{C}[\bm{x}]\,[[\bm{y}/\bm{x}]]\,[\bm{y}^{-1}]\;\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}\,\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})}

of formal series (for any
{\mu}
) via

\displaystyle x\mapsto\bm{x},\qquad y\mapsto\bm{y},\qquad\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}x}\mapsto\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}\bm{x}},\qquad\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}y}\mapsto\frac{{\partial}}{{\partial}\bm{y}}

and the factors
(-x-y)^{n}

are expanded as the following formal power series

\displaystyle(-x-y)^{n}\;\mapsto\;(-\bm{x}-\bm{y})^{n}\,=\,(-\bm{x})^{n}\,\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\binom{n}{k}\,(\bm{y}/\bm{x})^{k}.

These differential operators
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}
and
\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{n}^{(2)}

are obtained from
\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}
and
\widetilde{\mathscr{L}}_{n}^{(2)}
by changing
variables from
x_{1},x_{2}
to
x=x_{1},y=x_{2}-x_{1}
. In particular the
following is obvious from the
property
\widetilde{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}\,\mathcal{F}[\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}}](0,x_{1},x_{2})\,=\,0
,
which itself is obtained from the (PDE) part of
Theorem
2.2
using
Lemma
5.6
.

Lemma 6.3
.

For any
{\mu}
we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}\,\mathcal{F}[\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}}](0,x,x+y)\;=\;0.

Next we show the analoguous property of the
formal series.

Proposition 6.4
.

For each
{\mu}\in\mathrm{Sel}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\cap\mathrm{Sel}(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{\infty})
,
the formal series

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{\underline{{\lambda}}}_{{\mu}}(\bm{x},\bm{y}):=\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}},\bm{y})\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},\bm{x}\big)\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}}

lies in
\mathbb{C}[[\bm{y}/\bm{x}]]\,\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})}\,\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}
,
and it satisfies the differential equation

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}\,\widehat{C}^{\underline{{\lambda}}}_{{\mu}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})\;=\;0.

Proof.

We expand the intertwining operator
\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}},\bm{y})
so that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}},\bm{y})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})-n-1}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}})_{(n)},

where each
(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}})_{(n)}\in\mathrm{Hom}(Q_{\lambda_{1}},Q_{{\mu}})
is of degree
-n-1
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
In particular, we have
(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}})_{(n)}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}}\in Q_{{\mu}}(-n-1)
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, implying that
(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}})_{(n)}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}}=0
unless
n\leq-1
.
Furthermore, we expand each part
\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}((\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}})_{(n)}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},\bm{x})
,
n\leq-1
so that

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}((\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}})_{(n)}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},\bm{x})=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})-m-1}\left((\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}})_{(n)}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}}\right)_{(m)},

where
\left((\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}})_{(n)}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}}\right)_{(m)}\in\mathrm{Hom}(Q_{\lambda_{0}},Q_{\lambda_{\infty}})
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}
is of degree
-n-m-2
.
Therefore, the matrix element

\displaystyle\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\left((\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}})_{(n)}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}}\right)_{(m)}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}}

vanishes unless
-n-m-2=0
.
Consequently, we see that the series
\widehat{C}^{\underline{{\lambda}}}_{{\mu}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})

is expanded as

\displaystyle\widehat{C}^{\underline{{\lambda}}}_{{\mu}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})\;=\;\;

\displaystyle\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})}\,\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}\;\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\left((\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}})_{(-n-1)}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}}\right)_{(n-1)}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}}(\bm{y}/\bm{x})^{n}

\displaystyle\in\;\;

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[[\bm{y}/\bm{x}]]\,\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})}\,\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}.

The differential equation follows from the quotienting
out of the singular vector

S_{\lambda_{2}}\bar{w}_{c,h(\lambda_{2})}
of the Verma module
M(c,h(\lambda_{2}))

given by (
4.12
);
in
Q_{\lambda_{2}}

we have

S_{\lambda_{2}}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}}=0
and therefore

\displaystyle\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(S_{\lambda_{2}}\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}},\bm{y})\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},\,\bm{x}\big)\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}}\;=\;0.

To see that this gives the differential equation of the asserted form,
the key is to observe that
for any
w_{2}\in Q_{\lambda_{2}}
and
p>0
, we have

\displaystyle\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(L_{-p}w_{2},\bm{y})\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},\bm{x}\big)\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}}\;=\;\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{-p}^{(2)}\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},\,\bm{x}\big)\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}},

which in turn follows by a calculation based on the formulas of
Corollary
4.7
. The calculation is otherwise
very similar to
that in the proof of Lemma
5.4
,
except that part (a) of Corollary
4.7
has to be used
twice here.
∎

By the chosen normalization of the intertwining operators,
the coefficient of
\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})}\,\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}
in

\widehat{C}^{\underline{{\lambda}}}_{{\mu}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})
is

B_{{{\mu}},{\lambda_{0}}}^{\;{\,\lambda_{\infty}}}\,B_{{\lambda_{1}},{\lambda_{2}}}^{\;{\;{\mu}}}
.
In particular,
\widehat{C}^{\underline{{\lambda}}}_{{\mu}}(\bm{x},\bm{y})

is non-zero.

The remaining core ingredient is a suitable uniqueness
statement for series form solutions of the PDE.
Two aspects of the statement here are worth noting.
First, we will have a uniqueness statement separately for
every
{\mu}
, in an appropriate space of formal series.
Different
{\mu}
would give other linearly independent solutions,
but due to different characteristic exponents, the forms of the series
are different.
Second, although we require just
one differential equation, we obtain uniqueness, because
we additionally fix the total homogeneity degree. Alternatively
it would be possible to start without the homogeneity requirement,
using instead further differential equations that can also
be established in the present case.

Proposition 6.5
.

For each
{\mu}\in\mathrm{Sel}(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\cap\mathrm{Sel}(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{\infty})
,
subspace

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ker}\,\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}\;\subset\;\mathbb{C}[[\bm{y}/\bm{x}]]\;\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}\,\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})}

consisting of the solutions to the above differential
equation is one-dimensional,

\displaystyle\mathrm{dim}\Big(\mathrm{Ker}\,\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}\Big)\;=\;1.

Proof.

Since
\widehat{C}^{\underline{{\lambda}}}_{{\mu}}
is a non-zero solution
in this space of formal series,
it suffices to show that solutions are unique up to multiplicative constant.

For the present purpose, we introduce a
\mathbb{Z}
-grading of the space

\mathbb{C}[\bm{x}]\,[[\bm{y}/\bm{x}]]\,[\bm{y}^{-1}]\;\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}\,\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})}
:
for
n,m\in\mathbb{Z}
, a monomial

\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})+n}\,\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})+m}
is
declared
to be of degree
n
.
The degrees of operators are determined accordingly.

For each
p>0
,
the operator

\displaystyle\widehat{\underline{\mathscr{L}}}_{{-p}}^{({2})}\;=\;-(-\bm{y})^{-p}\,\left(\bm{y}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bm{y}}}+(1-p)\,h({\lambda_{1}})\right)

is of degree
-p
, and the difference

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{L}}_{-p}^{(2)}-\widehat{\underline{\mathscr{L}}}_{{-p}}^{({2})}

\displaystyle=\;\;

\displaystyle-(-\bm{y})^{-p+1}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bm{x}}}+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\binom{-p+1}{k}\,(-1)^{p+1}\,\bm{y}^{k}\,\bm{x}^{-p-k}\,\Big(\bm{x}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bm{x}}}+(1-p-k)\,h(\lambda_{0})\Big)

is a sum of terms of degrees strictly greater than
-p
.
Therefore, we can decompose the action of
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}

on
\mathbb{C}[\bm{x}]\,[[\bm{y}/\bm{x}]]\,[\bm{y}^{-1}]\;\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}\,\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})}
so that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}\;=\;\widehat{\underline{\mathscr{D}}}^{(2)}{}+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\widehat{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}_{k},

where
\deg\widehat{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}_{k}=-\lambda_{2}-1+k
for
k\geq 1
, and

\displaystyle\widehat{\underline{\mathscr{D}}}^{(2)}{}=\sum_{k=1}^{\lambda_{2}+1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\dots,p_{k}\geq 1\\
p_{1}+\cdots+p_{k}=\lambda_{2}+1\end{subarray}}\frac{(-4/\kappa)^{1+\lambda_{2}-k}\lambda_{2}!}{\prod_{u=1}^{k-1}(\sum_{i=1}^{u}p_{i})(\sum_{i=u+1}^{k}p_{i})}\,\widehat{\underline{\mathscr{L}}}_{{-p_{1}}}^{({2})}\cdots\widehat{\underline{\mathscr{L}}}_{{-p_{k}}}^{({2})}

is a differential operator of degree
-\lambda_{2}-1
.

We also have

\displaystyle\widehat{\underline{\mathscr{D}}}^{(2)}{}\ \bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})+n}\;=\;P_{\lambda_{2}}\big(h(\lambda_{1}),h(\mu)+n\big)\;\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})+n-\lambda_{2}-1}

for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and we recall that

P_{\lambda_{2}}(h(\lambda_{1}),h(\mu)+n)=0
if and only if
n=0
.

Let us expand a series
\widehat{C}\in\mathbb{C}[[\bm{y}/\bm{x}]]\;\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}\,\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})}
so that

\displaystyle\widehat{C}\;=\;\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\widehat{C}_{n}\;\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})-n}\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})+n}.

Requiring the differential equation
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}\,\widehat{C}=0

and considering the terms of different degrees separately,
we obtain the recursion relations that determines all higher
coefficients
\widehat{C}_{k}
,
k>0
, by

\displaystyle\widehat{C}_{k}\;=\;

\displaystyle-\frac{\bm{y}^{-\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})-k+\lambda_{2}+1}\bm{x}^{-\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})+k}}{P_{\lambda_{2}}\big(h(\lambda_{1}),h(\mu)+k\big)}\;\sum_{n=0}^{k-1}\widehat{\mathscr{R}}^{(2)}_{k-n}\left(\bm{y}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}({\mu})+n}\,\bm{x}^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})-n}\,\widehat{C}_{n}\right).

Therefore, a solution is uniquely determined by its leading
coefficient
\widehat{C}_{0}
.
∎

Putting the above ingredients together, we can
prove Proposition
6.2
.

Proof of Proposition
6.2
.

By linearity on
\mathcal{F}
and decomposition of
u_{\underline{{\varsigma}}}
, we found

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[u_{\underline{{\varsigma}}}](0,x_{1},x_{2})\;=\;\sum_{{\mu}}\Big\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\lambda_{2}}&{\lambda_{1}}&{{\mu}}\\
{\lambda_{0}}&{\lambda_{\infty}}&{{\varsigma}}\end{array}\Big\}\;\mathcal{F}[\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}}](0,x_{1},x_{2}).

According to Theorem
5.9
,
the left hand side is given by the series

\displaystyle\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,{\varsigma}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}},x_{2})\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{1}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;{\varsigma}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},x_{1})\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}}.

On the right hand side, change variables to
x=x_{1},\,y=x_{2}-x_{1}
,
and recall from Lemma
6.3

that each term

\mathcal{F}[\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}}](0,x,x+y)

satisfies the differential equation

\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}\,\mathcal{F}[\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}}](0,x,x+y)=0

and by Lemma
2.5

has a Frobenius series of the form (
6.3
).
By homogeneity, property (COV) in Theorem
2.2
,
it is easy to see that the coefficients are of the
form
\widehat{C}_{n}(x)=c_{n}\,x^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})-n}
.
The power series part of the Frobenius series in variable
y

is locally uniformly

R
-controlled in the domain defined by
x>R
, so
by arguments similar to the proof of
Theorem
5.9

we get that the differential
operator
\widehat{\mathscr{D}}^{(2)}
acts naturally coefficientwise on the series.
The uniqueness up to multiplicative constant of series solutions stated in
Proposition
6.5

then shows that the series
expansion is

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}[\widehat{u}_{\underline{{\mu}}}](0,x,x+y)\;=\;\Braket{\bar{w}_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime},\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(\bar{w}_{\lambda_{2}},y)\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{1}},x\big)\,\bar{w}_{\lambda_{0}}},

since the leading coefficients on both sides are

\widehat{C}_{0}(x)=B_{{\lambda_{2}},{\lambda_{1}}}^{\;{\;\;{\mu}}}\,B_{{{\mu}},{\lambda_{0}}}^{\;{\,\lambda_{\infty}}}\;x^{\widehat{{\Delta}}^{\prime}({\mu})}
.
∎

6.3.
Reduction to the initial terms

Here, we present a proof of Theorem
6.1
.
The proof is by induction on the total PBW length,
broadly similarly to Proposition
4.12
.
In fact, since the proof splits to many similar cases, we only provide the details
about one. Proposition
6.2
from above
will serve as the base case of the induction.

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Recall that all the modules
Q_{\lambda_{i}}
,
i=0,1,2
and
Q_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime}
admit the PBW filtration;

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}^{0}Q_{\lambda_{i}}\;\subset\;\cdots

\displaystyle\;\subset\;\mathscr{F}^{p_{i}}Q_{\lambda_{i}}\;\subset\;\mathscr{F}^{p_{i}+1}Q_{\lambda_{i}}\;\subset\;\cdots,\qquad\text{ for }i=0,1,2,

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}^{0}Q_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime}\;\subset\;\cdots

\displaystyle\;\subset\;\mathscr{F}^{p_{\infty}}Q_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime}\;\subset\;\mathscr{F}^{p_{\infty}+1}Q_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime}\;\subset\;\cdots.

Fix
p\in\mathbb{N}
and assume that the claim in Theorem
6.1

is true for any
w_{i}\in\mathscr{F}^{p_{i}}Q_{\lambda_{i}}
,
i=0,1,2

and
w_{\infty}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}^{p_{\infty}}Q_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime}

with
p_{0}+p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{\infty}\leq p
.
Note also that the base
case
p_{0}=p_{1}=p_{2}=p_{\infty}=0
is covered
by Proposition
6.2
.

Let us first consider increasing
p_{0}
by one, by
applying
L_{-n}
,
n>0
, on
w_{0}\in\mathscr{F}^{p_{0}}Q_{\lambda_{0}}
.
The Jacobi identity, as formulated in
Corollary
4.7
, yields the following

\displaystyle\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,{\varsigma}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}(w_{2},\bm{x}_{2})\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{1}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;{\varsigma}}(w_{1},\bm{x}_{1})\,L_{-n}w_{0}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\Braket{L_{n}w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,{\varsigma}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}(w_{2},\bm{x}_{2})\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{1}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;{\varsigma}}(w_{1},\bm{x}_{1})\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle-\bm{x}_{1}^{-n+1}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bm{x}_{1}}}\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,{\varsigma}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}(w_{2},\bm{x}_{2})\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{1}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;{\varsigma}}(w_{1},\bm{x}_{1})\;w_{0}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\binom{-n+1}{k}\bm{x}_{1}^{-n-k+1}\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,{\varsigma}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}(w_{2},\bm{x}_{2})\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{1}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;{\varsigma}}(L_{k-1}w_{1},\bm{x}_{1})\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle-\bm{x}_{2}^{-n+1}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bm{x}_{2}}}\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,{\varsigma}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}(w_{2},\bm{x}_{2})\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{1}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;{\varsigma}}(w_{1},\bm{x}_{1})\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\binom{-n+1}{k}\bm{x}_{2}^{-n-k+1}\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,{\varsigma}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}(L_{k-1}w_{2},\bm{x}_{2})\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{1}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;{\varsigma}}(w_{1},\bm{x}_{1})\,w_{0}}.

Each of the terms of the right hand side have a
total PBW word length
\leq p
,
so the induction hypothesis can be applied to each of them.
By the induction hypothesis, the right hand side power series represents the same
analytic function as the following series

\displaystyle\sum_{{\mu}}\Big\{\begin{array}[]{ccc}{\lambda_{2}}&{\lambda_{1}}&{{\mu}}\\
{\lambda_{0}}&{\lambda_{\infty}}&{{\varsigma}}\end{array}\Big\}\bigg(\,

\displaystyle\Braket{L_{n}w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle-\bm{x}^{1-n}\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bm{x}}}-\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bm{y}}}\right)\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x})\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\binom{1-n}{k}\bm{x}^{1-n-k}\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})L_{k-1}w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle-(\bm{x}+\bm{y})^{1-n}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bm{y}}}\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

(6.8)

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\binom{1-n}{k}(\bm{x}+\bm{y})^{1-n-k}\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(L_{k-1}w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}\bigg)

when the two series are evaluated at
\bm{x}_{1}=x_{1}
,
\bm{x}_{2}=x_{2}
, and

\bm{x}=x_{1}
,
\bm{y}=x_{2}-x_{1}
, respectively.
It remains to check that the expression inside the parentheses
in (
6.8
) coincides with

\displaystyle\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,L_{-n}w_{0}}.

For this, we first commute
L_{-n}
to the left with the
formula of Corollary
4.7
,

\displaystyle\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,L_{-n}w_{0}}

\displaystyle=\;\;

\displaystyle\Braket{L_{n}\,w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\binom{1-n}{\ell}\,\bm{x}^{1-n-\ell}\;\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(L_{\ell-1}\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

The first term here is indeed present
in (
6.8
),
so we focus on the second term. Also the
\ell=0

contribution in the second term is

\displaystyle-\bm{x}^{1-n}\,\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(L_{-1}\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle\;=\;\;

\displaystyle-\bm{x}^{1-n}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bm{x}}}\,\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}},

which also appears in (
6.8
).
We rewrite the remaining terms by commuting
L_{\ell-1}
to the right inside
the intertwiner, leading to a contribution

\displaystyle-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\binom{1-n}{\ell}\,\bm{x}^{1-n-\ell}\;\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(L_{\ell-1}\,\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle\;=\;\;

\displaystyle-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\binom{1-n}{\ell}\,\bm{x}^{1-n-\ell}\Bigg(\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})L_{\ell-1}\,w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\binom{\ell}{k}\,\bm{y}^{\ell-k}\;\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(L_{k-1}w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}\Bigg)

Again the first term appears
in (
6.8
), so it remains
to treat the last term. The
k=0
contribution of it is just

\displaystyle-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\binom{1-n}{\ell}\,\bm{x}^{1-n-\ell}\,\bm{y}^{\ell}\;\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(L_{-1}w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle\;=\;\;

\displaystyle-(\bm{x}+\bm{y})^{1-n}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bm{y}}}\,\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle+\bm{x}^{1-n}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial{\bm{y}}}\,\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}.

Both of these again appear
in (
6.8
).
In the remaining terms, we interchange the order of summations
and change to a new summation variable
m=\ell-k
to express them as

\displaystyle-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\infty}\binom{1-n}{\ell}\,\bm{x}^{1-n-\ell}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\binom{\ell}{k}\,\bm{y}^{\ell-k}\;\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(L_{k-1}w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle=\;\;

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell=k}^{\infty}\binom{\ell}{k}\,\binom{1-n}{\ell}\,\bm{x}^{1-n-\ell}\,\bm{y}^{\ell-k}\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(L_{k-1}w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}

\displaystyle=\;\;

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{k!\,m!}\prod_{i=0}^{k+m-1}(1-n-i)\;\,\bm{x}^{1-n-k-m}\,\bm{y}^{m}\Braket{w_{\infty}^{\prime},\;\mathcal{Y}_{{\mu}\,\lambda_{0}}^{\;\lambda_{\infty}}\big(\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda_{2}\,\lambda_{1}}^{\;{\mu}}(L_{k-1}w_{2},\bm{y})w_{1},\bm{x}\big)\,w_{0}}.

This equals the remaining term
in (
6.8
).

There are in principle three more cases to consider:
the application of
L_{-n}
on
w_{1}\in\mathscr{F}^{p_{1}}Q_{\lambda_{1}}
,
on
w_{2}\in\mathscr{F}^{p_{2}}Q_{\lambda_{2}}
, and
on
w_{\infty}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{F}^{p_{\infty}}Q_{\lambda_{\infty}}^{\prime}
.
The calculations are similar to the case above, so we omit the details here.
∎

Appendix A
Proofs of analyticity and Frobenius series

This appendix is devoted to indicating how the
results of
[
KP20
]

can be strengthened so as to yield the analyticity
and series expansion properties needed in the present
article, and for some elementary power series estimates
employed in the proofs of the main results in
Sections
5
and
6
.

A.1.
Elementary power series estimates

In Sections
5.4

and
6.2
,
we needed that three types of operations preserved a suitable
space of parametrized power series, and that these
operations could be performed essentially “coefficientwise”.
Specifically, we needed

•

differentiation of the power series itself;

•

multiplication of the power series by another power series;

•

differentiation of the power series with respect to parameters.

The setup is that we consider power series in a variable
z
, of the form

(A.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c_{k}(y)\,z^{k},

where the coefficients
c_{k}
depend smoothly on parameters
y\in\Omega
,
where
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
is an open set. The main assumption is that
for some
R>0
, the parametrized power series is locally uniformly
R
-controlled
in the sense of Definition
2.3
.
For concretely working with the power series, it is useful to note
that the property of being locally uniformly
R
-controlled
is equivalent to the following: for
every compact
K\subset\Omega
, every multi-index
\alpha
,
and every
0<R_{0}<R
, there exists a
M<\infty
such that

(A.2)

\displaystyle\big|\partial^{\alpha}c_{k}(y)\big|\;\leq\;M\,R_{0}^{-k}\qquad\text{for all $y\in K$ and $k\in\mathbb{N}$.}

This in particular ensures that for every
y\in\Omega
, the radius
of convergence of (
A.1
) is at least
R
,
and for any
R_{0}<R
and any compact subset
K\subset\Omega
,
the convergence is uniform over
|z|\leq R_{0}
and
y\in K
.

Crucial for us is that the locally uniformly
R
-controlled
power series are stable under
differentiation of the power series, multiplication by another
locally uniformly
R
-controlled power series,
and differentiation with respect to the parameters
y\in\Omega
.
The first two are in essence just familiar operations
on ordinary power series — only the last one involves
dependence on the parameters
y
. The proofs are all elementary.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{m}
be an open set, and let
R>0
.

(a)

If
(c_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are locally uniformly
R
-controlled,
then also
{\big((k+1)c_{k+1}\big)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}}
are
locally uniformly
R
-controlled and for any
z\in\mathbb{C}

with
|z|<R
and any
y\in\Omega
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}{z}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c_{k}(y)\,z^{k}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(k+1)\,c_{k+1}(y)\,z^{k}.

(b)

If
(c_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(d_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are locally
uniformly
R
-controlled,
then also the convoluted coefficients

{\big(\sum_{j=0}^{k}c_{k-j}d_{j}\big)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}}
are
locally uniformly
R
-controlled and for any
z\in\mathbb{C}

with
|z|<R
and any
y\in\Omega
we have

\displaystyle\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}d_{k}(y)\,z^{k}\Big)\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c_{k}(y)\,z^{k}\Big)=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{k}c_{k-j}(y)\,d_{j}(y)\bigg)\,z^{k}.

(c)

If
(c_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
are locally uniformly
R
-controlled
and
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,m\right\}
,
then also the coefficients

{\big(\partial_{j}c_{k}\big)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}}
are
locally uniformly
R
-controlled and for any
z\in\mathbb{C}

with
|z|<R
and any
y\in\Omega
we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial{y_{j}}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c_{k}(y)\,z^{k}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\partial_{j}c_{k})(y)\,z^{k}.

Proof.

The statements (a) and (b) at a fixed parameter
y

are textbook power series results based on the
estimates (
A.2
),
and the explicit calculations yield locally
uniformly
R
-controlledness by the same characterization.

For property (c), note first that arbitrary
partial derivatives of the coefficients satisfy the same
estimate (
A.2
),
so it is clear that any partial derivatives of the coefficients
remain locally uniformly
R
-controlled.
It suffices to check that the the partial derivative of the
series is the series with partial derivative coefficients.
Fix
y\in\Omega
and choose a small
r>0
so that the closed ball

\overline{B}_{r}(y)\subset\Omega
.
First fix
R^{\prime}<R_{0}<R
and
consider
|z|\leq R^{\prime}
. Since
\overline{B}_{r}(y)\subset\Omega
is compact,
we may choose
M<\infty

such that for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
and
y\in\overline{B}_{r}(u)
we have
both
|c_{k}(y)|\leq M\,R_{0}^{-k}
and

\big|\partial_{j}c_{k}(y)\big|\leq M\,R_{0}^{-k}
.
The partial derivative of the power series
with respect to the
j
:th parameter
y_{j}
at
y=(y_{1},\ldots,y_{m})

is

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial{y_{j}}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c_{k}(y)\,z^{k}=\;

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\;\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{c_{k}(y+\delta\hat{e}_{j})-c_{k}(y)}{\delta}\,z^{k}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\lim_{\delta\to 0}\;\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big(\int_{0}^{1}(\partial_{j}c_{k})(y+s\delta\hat{e}_{j})\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)\,z^{k}.

For
\delta<r
the integral above is bounded by the same
M\,R_{0}^{-k}

(since the integrand is, and the integration is taken over the unit integral),
so for
|z|\leq R^{\prime}<R_{0}
the power series terms are dominated by

M\,\big(\frac{R^{\prime}}{R_{0}}\big)^{k}
, which are summable.
Moreover, as
\delta\to 0
, the integrands are tending pointwise w.r.t.
s
to
the constant
(\partial_{j}c_{k})(y)
, and they are bounded by the above.
With these observations, we can apply dominated convergence
to interchange the limit
\delta\to 0

with both the series and the interal, and the assertion (c) follows.
∎

A.2.
Power series for the functions from the quantum group method

We now outline the proofs of
Lemmas
2.4

and
2.5
.
For convenience, we also recall their statements here.

Lemma

(Lemma
2.4
)
.

Let
F=\mathcal{F}[u]\colon\mathfrak{X}_{N}\to\mathbb{C}

be the function associated to
any
u\in\mathcal{H}_{\underline{{\lambda}}}
,
and let
(x_{1},\ldots,x_{N})\in\mathfrak{X}_{N}
,
and let
j\in\left\{1,\ldots,N\right\}
.
Then we have a power series expansion

\displaystyle F(x_{1},\ldots,x_{j-1},z_{j},x_{j+1},\ldots,x_{N})\;=\;\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c_{k}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{j-1},x_{j+1},\ldots,x_{N})\;\big(z_{j}-x_{j}\big)^{k}

in the
j
:th variable.
For fixed
x_{j}\in\mathbb{R}
and
R>0
,
viewing the other variables
(x_{i})_{i\neq j}
as parameters,
on the subset
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{N-1}

defined by the conditions
x_{1}<\cdots<x_{N}

and
\min_{i\neq j}|x_{i}-x_{j}|>R
, the power series
is locally uniformly
R
-controlled.

Proof.

The construction
in
[
KP20
]

expresses
F=\mathcal{F}[u]
as a finite linear
combination of integrals

\displaystyle G(x_{1},\ldots,x_{N})=\idotsint_{\Gamma}f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{N};w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\;\mathrm{d}w_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}w_{\ell},

where the integrands are branches of the multivalued function

\displaystyle f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{N};w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\mathrm{const.}\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq N}(x_{j}-x_{i})^{\frac{2}{\kappa}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq N\\
1\leq r\leq\ell\end{subarray}}(x_{i}-w_{r})^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\lambda_{i}}\prod_{1\leq r<s\leq\ell}(w_{s}-w_{r})^{\frac{8}{\kappa}},

and where the integration surfaces
\Gamma
are
collections of non-intersecting loops
(for details
see
[
KP20
]
).
Most importantly, the integration surfaces
\Gamma
are
compact, and the integrands
(w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\mapsto f(x_{1},\ldots,x_{N};w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})
are continuous
on them.

Fix
R>0
and a compact subset
K\subset\Omega_{R}=\left\{(x_{i})_{i\neq j}\in\mathbb{R}^{N-1}\;\big|\;\min_{i\neq j}|x_{i}-x_{j}|>R\right\}
.
When
(x_{i})_{i\neq j}\in K
,
without changing the homotopy class
of
\Gamma
, it is possible to arrange so that
none of the
\ell
coordinates of
\Gamma=\Gamma_{K}

intersects the closed ball
\overline{B}_{R}(x_{j})

of radius
R
centered at
x_{j}
.
For notational convenience,
let us keep the coordinates
x_{i}
,
i\neq j
,
fixed and omit them from the notation, and
consider only the dependence
of the integrand
f

on the
j
:th variable
(denoted
z_{j}
; we will perform power series
expansions around
z_{j}=x_{j}
)
and the integration variables
w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell}
.
From the explicit formula for
f
,
it is clear that for any
(w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\in\Gamma
,
the function
z_{j}\mapsto f(z_{j};w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})

is analytic in an open set
containing
\overline{B}_{R}(x_{j})
,
and has a convergent power series
expansion

\displaystyle f(z_{j};w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\phi_{k}(w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\,(z_{j}-x_{j})^{k},

where the Taylor coefficients
obey the Cauchy integral based estimates

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{k}(w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\big|\leq\frac{\max_{z_{j}\in\overline{B}_{R}(x_{j})}\big|f(z_{j};w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\big|}{R^{k}}.

In particular we get the uniform estimate

\displaystyle\big|\phi_{k}(w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\big|\leq\frac{C_{K}}{R^{k}},

for all
(w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\in\Gamma
,
with the finite constant
C_{K}
taken as the
maximum of
\big|f(z_{j};w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\big|

over the compact
set
\overline{B}_{R}(x_{j})\times K\times\Gamma_{K}

(the middle factor is for the implicit parameters
x_{i}
,
i\neq j
).
Then for

\displaystyle G(z_{j})=\idotsint_{\Gamma_{K}}f(z_{j};w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\;\mathrm{d}w_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}w_{\ell},

we can use a power series expansion with coefficients

\displaystyle c_{k}:=\idotsint_{\Gamma_{K}}\phi_{k}(w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\;\mathrm{d}w_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}w_{\ell},

which
satisfy
|c_{k}|\leq C_{K}\,|\Gamma_{K}|\,R^{-k}
.
The series converges to
G(z_{j})

whenever
{|z_{j}-x_{j}|<R}
, by virtue of the error term estimate

\displaystyle\Big|G(z_{j})-\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}c_{k}\,(z_{j}-x_{j})^{k}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\idotsint_{\Gamma_{K}}\Big|f(z_{j};w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})-\sum_{k=0}^{k_{0}}\phi_{k}(w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\,(z_{j}-x_{j})^{k}\Big|\;\mathrm{d}w_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}w_{\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle C_{K}\,|\Gamma_{K}|\;\sum_{k=k_{0}+1}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{|z_{j}-x_{j}|}{R}\Big)^{k}\;\underset{k_{0}\to\infty}{\longrightarrow}\;0.

From the above explicit estimate for coefficients
(c_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
, we see
that
z_{j}\mapsto G(z_{j})
is given by a locally
uniformly
R
-controlled power series parametrized by
\Omega_{R}
.
The same holds for the
finite linear combination
z_{j}\mapsto F(z_{j})

of such terms.
∎

The Frobenius series statement that we will use
is the following. Variants of this formulation
with obvious modifications to the statement and
proof could be done as well.

Lemma

(Lemma
2.5
)
.

Let
j\in\left\{2,\ldots,N\right\}
.
Suppose that
\tau\in\mathrm{Sel}(\lambda_{j-1},\lambda_{j})

and that
u\in\mathcal{H}_{\underline{{\lambda}}}

is such that
u={\pi}_{\{{j-1},{j}\}}^{\;\;{\tau}}(u)
.
The function

F=\mathcal{F}[u]\colon\mathfrak{X}_{N}\to\mathbb{C}

associated to
u
has a Frobenius series
expansion in the
variable
z=x_{j}-x_{j-1}

\displaystyle F\big(x_{1},\ldots,x_{j-1},(x_{j-1}+z),x_{j+1},\ldots,x_{N}\big)\;=\;z^{{\Delta}}\,\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}c_{k}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{j-1},x_{j+1},\ldots,x_{N})\;z^{k}

where
the indicial exponent is

{\Delta}=h(\tau)-h(\lambda_{j})-h(\lambda_{j-1})
.
For fixed
R>0
,
viewing the other variables
(x_{i})_{i\neq j}
as parameters,
on the subset
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{N-1}

defined by the conditions
x_{1}<\cdots<x_{N}

and
\min_{i\neq j,j-1}|x_{i}-x_{j-1}|>R
, the power series
part of this Frobenius series is locally uniformly
R
-controlled,
and for
0<z<R
the Frobenius series converges to the function
F

on the left hand side.

Proof.

The idea of the proof is otherwise similar to
that of Lemma
2.4
,
except that a part of the integration surface needs to
be separated from the rest and that part of the surface
also undergoes scaling proportional to
variable
x_{j}-x_{j-1}
of the Frobenius series.

Again for notational simplicity, we
suppress the fixed variables
x_{i}
,
i\neq j
,
from the notation.
By translation invariance we can moreover
assume
x_{j-1}=0
, so that the
Frobenius series variable is simply
x_{j}

and notation with scalings centered
at
x_{j-1}
simplifies.

With the methods of
[
KP20
, Sections 3.4, 4.2,
and 4.3]
,
the assumption

u={\pi}_{\{{j-1},{j}\}}^{\;\;{\tau}}(u)
implies that
the function
x_{j}\mapsto F(x_{j})=\mathcal{F}[u](\ldots,x_{j},\ldots)

can be written as a finite
linear combination of integrals

\displaystyle H(x_{j})=\idotsint_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\bigg(\idotsint_{x_{j}\Gamma_{0}}f(x_{j};w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m},w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\;\mathrm{d}w^{\prime}_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}w^{\prime}_{m}\bigg)\mathrm{d}w_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}w_{\ell},

where the surface
\Gamma^{\prime}
is compact and can be kept
fixed as
x_{j}\downarrow 0
,
and where and
x_{j}\Gamma_{0}
stands for a scaling
by a factor
x_{j}>0
of a fixed surface
\Gamma_{0}

(same for all terms in the linear combination
and for all sufficiently small
x_{j}>0
) of
dimension
m=\frac{1}{2}\big(\lambda_{j-1}+\lambda_{j}-\tau\big)
,
and the integrand

f(x_{j};w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m},w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})

is as in
Lemma
2.4
.
(now all of

w^{\prime}_{1},\ldots,w^{\prime}_{m},w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell}

are in the same role as
w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell}
originally).
With a change of variables to unit scale,

t_{r}=w^{\prime}_{r}/x_{j}
for
r=1,\ldots,m
,
both integration surfaces become constant, and the
dependence on
x_{j}
is entirely in the integrand:
we find

\displaystyle H(x_{j})=\idotsint_{\Gamma^{\prime}}\bigg(\idotsint_{\Gamma_{0}}x_{j}^{m}\,f(x_{j};x_{j}t_{1},\ldots,x_{j}t_{m},w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})\;\mathrm{d}t_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}t_{m}\bigg)\mathrm{d}w_{1}\cdots\mathrm{d}w_{\ell}.

We will compare

f(x_{j};x_{j}t_{1},\ldots,x_{j}t_{m},w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})

with (we omit all of the fixed arguments

x_{i}
,
i\neq j
, for brevity)

\displaystyle\hat{f}(\ ;w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\mathrm{const.}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i<i^{\prime}\leq N:\\
i,i^{\prime}\neq j\end{subarray}}(x_{i^{\prime}}-x_{i})^{\frac{2}{\kappa}\hat{\lambda}_{i}\hat{\lambda}_{i^{\prime}}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq N:\;i\neq j\\
1\leq r\leq\ell\end{subarray}}(x_{i}-w_{r})^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\hat{\lambda}_{i}}\prod_{1\leq r<s\leq\ell}(w_{s}-w_{r})^{\frac{8}{\kappa}},

where

\displaystyle\hat{\lambda}_{i}=\begin{cases}\tau&\text{ if $i=j-1$.}\\
\lambda_{i}&\text{ if $i\neq j-1,j$,}\end{cases}

because

\hat{f}(\ ;w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})
is the integrand
whose integral on
\Gamma^{\prime}
yields the asserted
leading asymptotic coefficient.

The ratio

\frac{f(x_{j};x_{j}t_{1},\ldots,x_{j}t_{m},w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})}{\hat{f}(\ ;w_{1},\ldots,w_{\ell})}

contains factors (we set
x_{j-1}=0
)

\displaystyle(x_{j}-0)^{\frac{2}{\kappa}\lambda_{j-1}\lambda_{j}}\;=\;\;

\displaystyle x_{j}^{\frac{2}{\kappa}\lambda_{j-1}\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle(x_{j}t_{a}-0)^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\lambda_{j-1}}\;=\;\;

\displaystyle x_{j}^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\lambda_{j-1}}\;(t_{a}-0)^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\lambda_{j-1}}

\displaystyle\text{for }1\leq a\leq m

\displaystyle(x_{j}t_{a}-x_{j})^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\lambda_{j}}\;=\;\;

\displaystyle x_{j}^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\lambda_{j}}\;(t_{a}-1)^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\lambda_{j}}

\displaystyle\text{for }1\leq a\leq m

\displaystyle(x_{j}t_{b}-x_{j}t_{a})^{\frac{8}{\kappa}}\;=\;\;

\displaystyle x_{j}^{\frac{8}{\kappa}}\;(t_{b}-t_{a})^{\frac{8}{\kappa}}

\displaystyle\text{for }1\leq a<b\leq m,

out of which we extract in particular the powers
of the scaling factor
x_{j}
, which combined with the
factor
x_{j}^{m}
from the change of variables produce
the correct overall scaling by

\displaystyle x_{j}^{m+\frac{2}{\kappa}\lambda_{j-1}\lambda_{j}-\frac{4}{\kappa}(\lambda_{j-1}+\lambda_{j})m+\frac{8}{\kappa}m(m-1)/2}=x_{j}^{h(\tau)-h(\lambda_{j-1})-h(\lambda_{j})}=x_{j}^{\Delta}.

The
t_{a}
-dependent factors are yet
to be integrated over
\Gamma_{0}
.
The ratio can naturally also be rearranged
(by appropriately splitting the factor

(w_{r}-0)^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\hat{\lambda}_{j-1}}

in the denominator) to contain factors

\displaystyle\left(\frac{x_{i}-x_{j}}{x_{i}-0}\right)^{\frac{2}{\kappa}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}\;=\;\;

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{x_{j}}{x_{i}}\right)^{\frac{2}{\kappa}\lambda_{i}\lambda_{j}}=\;1+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}c_{k}(x_{i})\;x_{j}^{k}

\displaystyle\text{for }i\notin\left\{j-1,j\right\}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{x_{i}-x_{j}t_{a}}{x_{i}-0}\right)^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\lambda_{i}}\;=\;\;

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{x_{j}t_{a}}{x_{i}}\right)^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\lambda_{i}}=\;1+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}c_{k}(x_{i})\;t_{a}^{k}\;x_{j}^{k}

\displaystyle\text{for }i\notin\left\{j-1,j\right\}\text{, }1\leq a\leq m

\displaystyle\left(\frac{w_{r}-x_{j}}{w_{r}-0}\right)^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\lambda_{j}}\;=\;\;

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{x_{j}}{w_{r}}\right)^{-\frac{4}{\kappa}\lambda_{j}}=\;1+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}c_{k}(w_{r})\;x_{j}^{k}

\displaystyle\text{for }1\leq r\leq\ell

\displaystyle\left(\frac{w_{r}-x_{j}t_{a}}{w_{r}-0}\right)^{\frac{8}{\kappa}}\;=\;\;

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{x_{j}t_{a}}{w_{r}}\right)^{\frac{8}{\kappa}}=\;1+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}c_{k}(w_{r})\;t_{a}^{k}\;x_{j}^{k}

\displaystyle\text{for }1\leq r\leq\ell\text{, }1\leq a\leq m

which can be expanded
as power series in
x_{j}
with unit constant coefficient,
all having a radius of convergence at least a fixed positive
multiple of
the distance
R^{\prime}
of
x_{j-1}
to the contours in
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
when
(t_{1},\ldots,t_{m})
lies on
the compact set
\Gamma_{0}
.
All remaining factors in the ratio cancel directly.

We again conclude that after extracting the overall
scaling factor
x_{j}^{\Delta}
, the integrand
in
H(x_{j})
admits a power series expansion in
x_{j}
,
with the
k
:th coefficient bounded
by
p(k)(CR^{\prime})^{-k}
, where
p(k)
is a polynomial
and
C>0
is a fixed constant. By arguments similar
to the previous case, a term by term integration
of this yields a power series
for
x_{j}^{-\Delta}\,H(x_{j})
.
The proof of the existence of a convergent Frobenius
series expansion with some positive radius of convergence
is complete once one notices
that the integral of the constant coefficient
of the power series factorizes
to integrals over
\Gamma_{0}
and
\Gamma^{\prime}
,
the former yielding the beta-function coefficient
B

and the latter yielding the function with one fewer
variable and
labels
(\hat{\lambda}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq N:\,i\neq j}
.

The remaining part of the assertion is
the uniform
R
-controlledness on compact subsets
of the domain determined by
\min_{i\neq j,j-1}|x_{i}-x_{j-1}|>R
.
For any such compact subset, from the start we can choose

\Gamma^{\prime}
so that for some
\varepsilon>0
, on
\Gamma^{\prime}

we have
|w_{r}|\geq(1+\varepsilon)R
for all
r
, i.e.,
R^{\prime}\geq(1+\varepsilon)R
.
Moreover,
\Gamma_{0}
can be chosen so that on
\Gamma_{0}

we have
|t_{a}|\leq 1+\varepsilon/2
for all
a
. With such choices,
the constant above is
C\geq\frac{1}{1+\varepsilon/2}
, and the estimates
indeed yield uniform
R
-controlledness.
∎

Appendix B
Construction of the intertwining operators for Verma modules

Our goal is to show the following theorem (Theorem
4.15
):

Theorem B.1
.

For
h_{1},h_{0},h_{\infty}\in\mathbb{C}
,

\dim\mathcal{I}{\binom{M(c,h_{\infty})^{\prime}}{M(c,h_{1})\;M(c,h_{0})}}=1.

An intertwining operator in this case is unique up to multiplicative constants if exists (Proposition
4.12
).
Hence, we only have to prove the existence.
We apply the construction in
[
Li99
]
to the generic Virasoro VOA to obtain an intertwining operator among Verma modules; we include this so as
to be self-contained, and also because we believe that the concrete
case of the Virasoro VOA is instructive.
The procedure is divided into two parts: in the former part, we will construct a linear map

(B.1)

\mathcal{Y}\colon M(c,h_{1})\to\mathrm{Hom}(M(c,h_{0}),M(c,h_{\infty})^{\prime})\{\bm{x}\},

and in the latter part, we will show that this linear map
\mathcal{Y}
is indeed an intertwining operator of the desired type.

B.1.
Construction of a linear map

For convenience, we write
W_{0}:=M(c,h_{0})
,
W_{1}:=M(c,h_{1})
and
W_{\infty}:=M(c,h_{\infty})
.
For each
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we also write
W_{0}(k):=(W_{0})_{(h_{0}+k)}
,
W_{1}(k):=(W_{1})_{(h_{1}+k)}
and
W_{\infty}(k):=(W_{\infty})_{(h_{\infty}+k)}

for the eigenspaces of
L_{0}
.

Before constructing the linear map (
B.1
),
it is instructive to observe anticipated properties.
First of all, defining a linear map (
B.1
)
is equivalent to defining its matrix elements

W_{\infty}\times(W_{1}\otimes W_{0})\to\mathbb{C}\{\bm{x}\};\quad({w}_{\infty},{w}_{1}\otimes{w}_{0})\mapsto\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}.

Second, if
\mathcal{Y}
gives an intertwining operators
of the desired type, it is expanded as

\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x})=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}({w}_{1})_{(m)}\bm{x}^{\Delta-m-1},\quad\Delta=h_{\infty}-h_{1}-h_{0}.

Hence, for our purpose of constructing an intertwining operator,
it is natural to specify the linear map (
B.1
)
in terms of the family of infinitely many bilinear functionals

W_{\infty}\times(W_{1}\otimes W_{0})\to\mathbb{C};\quad({w}_{\infty},{w}_{1}\otimes{w}_{0})\mapsto\braket{{w}_{\infty},({w}_{1})_{(m)}{w}_{0}}

labeled by
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We will actually incorporate these bilinear functionals, by introducing the affinization
\widehat{W}_{1}:=\mathbb{C}[t^{\pm 1}]\otimes W_{1}
,
into a single bilinear functional

W_{\infty}\times(\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0})\to\mathbb{C};\quad({w}_{\infty},(t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0})\mapsto\braket{{w}_{\infty},({w}_{1})_{(m)}{w}_{0}}.

Finally, as we have seen in Proposition
4.12
,
an intertwining operator among highest weight modules is determined uniquely by the initial term.
Therefore, the desired bilinear functional should be determined recursively by the value at
(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},(t^{-1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{1}})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}})
.

Step 1

The first step is to fix an initial term.
Taking a constant
B\in\mathbb{C}\backslash\{0\}
,
we define a bilinear functional
5
5

5

At various stages of the construction, the superscripts to
\mathbb{M}

are meant to indicate in which of the modules

W_{0}
,
W_{1}
,
W_{\infty}

we restrict attention to only the one-dimensional subspaces

W_{0}(0)
,
W_{1}(0)
,
W_{\infty}(0)

of highest weight vectors.

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,1,0)}\colon W_{\infty}(0)\times(W_{1}(0)\otimes W_{0}(0))\to\mathbb{C}

by
\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,1,0)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\bar{w}_{c,h_{1}}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right):=B
.

Step 2

We extend the bilinear functional
\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,1,0)}

so that an arbitrary vector from
W_{1}
can be inserted.

Proposition B.2
.

There exists a unique bilinear functional

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}\colon W_{\infty}(0)\times(W_{1}\otimes W_{0}(0))\to\mathbb{C}

which coincides with
\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,1,0)}

on the subspace

\displaystyle W_{\infty}(0)\times(W_{1}(0)\otimes W_{0}(0))\;\subset\;W_{\infty}(0)\times(W_{1}\otimes W_{0}(0))

and which has the property that

\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{-n}{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}})=(-1)^{-n+1}(-h-nh_{0}+h_{\infty})\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}),

for any
n>0
and
{w}_{1}\in(W_{1})_{(h)}
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction with respect the PBW
filtration
(\mathscr{F}^{p}W_{1})_{p\in\mathbb{N}}
of
W_{1}
.
We recursively define

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{p}\colon W_{\infty}(0)\times(\mathscr{F}^{p}W_{1}\otimes W_{0}(0))\to\mathbb{C}

for
p\in\mathbb{N}
, and we show consistency and the desired
property.

The zeroth level of the filtration is just the highest
weight subspace,
\mathscr{F}^{0}W_{1}=W_{1}(0)
, so the
required coincidence with
\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,1,0)}

fully determines
\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{0}
, and provides the
base case for the recursion.

Suppose then
that
\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{p}\colon W_{\infty}(0)\times(\mathscr{F}^{p}W_{1}\otimes W_{0}(0))\to\mathbb{C}

are well-defined and consistent for
p\leq r-1
.
We want to
define
\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r}\colon W_{\infty}(0)\times(\mathscr{F}^{r}W_{1}\otimes W_{0}(0))\to\mathbb{C}
according to the required property, by setting

\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{-n}{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}):=(-1)^{-n+1}(-h-nh_{0}+h_{\infty})\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r-1}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}})

for
n>0
and
{w}_{1}\in\mathscr{F}^{r-1}W_{1}\cap(W_{1})_{(h)}
.
For well-definedness,
it suffices to show that

\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},(L_{-m}L_{-n}-L_{-n}L_{-m}-[L_{-m},L_{-n}]){w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)=0,

for any
m,n>0
and
{w}_{1}\in\mathscr{F}^{r-2}W_{1}
.
We may assume that
{w}_{1}\in(W_{1})_{(h)}
.
Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{-m}L_{-n}{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{-m-n}\left(-h-n-mh_{0}+h_{\infty}\right)\left(-h-nh_{0}+h_{\infty}\right)\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r-2}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}).

Hence we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},(L_{-m}L_{-n}-L_{-n}L_{-m}){w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{-m-n}\left((m-n)(-h+h_{\infty})-(m^{2}-n^{2})h_{0}\right)\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r-2}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}})

\displaystyle=(-m+n)(-1)^{-m-n+1}\left(-h-(m+n)h_{0}+h_{\infty}\right)\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r-2}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}})

\displaystyle=(-m+n)\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r-1}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{-m-n}{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}).

Therefore
\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r}
is well-defined.
Consistency is clear from the construction: for
s\leq r

the map
\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{r}

coincides with
\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}_{s}
on the
subspace
W_{\infty}(0)\times(\mathscr{F}^{s}W_{1}\otimes W_{0}(0))
.
∎

Step 3

As we anticipated, we now consider
the affinization of
W_{1}
,
\widehat{W}_{1}:=\mathbb{C}[t^{\pm 1}]\otimes W_{1}
.
Let us also introduce a
\mathbb{Z}
-grading of it as

\deg(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1}):=k-n-1\quad\mbox{when }{w}_{1}\in W_{1}(k),\quad n\in\mathbb{Z}.

We extend the bilinear functional
\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}
to

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}\colon W_{\infty}(0)\times(\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0}(0))\to\mathbb{C}

by

(B.2)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}}\otimes(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right):=\delta_{k-n-1,0}\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}}\otimes{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}})

for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}(k)
.
In particular,
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}
vanishes unless
t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1}\in\widehat{W}_{1}
is of degree
0
.

Step 4

The following fact will be used to
extend the bilinear functional
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}

so that arbitrary vectors from
W_{0}
can be inserted.

Proposition B.3
.

For
m,n\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
, we set

L_{m}(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1}):=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\binom{m+1}{k}t^{m+n+1-k}\otimes L_{k-1}{w}_{1},\quad C(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1}):=0.

Then, this gives a representation of the Virasoro algebra
of central charge
0
on
\widehat{W}_{1}
.
Furthermore,
L_{m}

has degree
-m
, i.e., when
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
is homogeneous, we have

\deg(L_{m}(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1}))=\deg(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})-m.

Proof.

Note that, for any
m,n,p\in\mathbb{Z}
and
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
,

\displaystyle L_{m}L_{n}(t^{p}\otimes{w}_{1})=\sum_{k,l=0}^{\infty}\binom{n+1}{k}\binom{m+1}{l}t^{m+n+p+2-k-l}\otimes L_{l-1}L_{k-1}{w}_{1}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle(L_{m}L_{n}-L_{n}L_{m})(t^{p}\otimes{w}_{1})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,l=0}^{\infty}\binom{n+1}{k}\binom{m+1}{l}t^{m+n+p+2-k-l}\otimes[L_{l-1},L_{k-1}]{w}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k,l=0}^{\infty}\binom{n+1}{k}\binom{m+1}{l}(l-k)t^{m+n+p+2-k-l}\otimes L_{l+k-2}{w}_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\binom{n+1}{k-l}\binom{m+1}{l}(l-(k-l))t^{m+n+p+2-k}\otimes L_{k-2}{w}_{1}

Here, we use the following identity:

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k}\binom{n+1}{k-l}\binom{m+1}{l}(l-(k-l))

\displaystyle=(m+1)\sum_{l=1}^{k}\binom{n+1}{k-l}\binom{m}{l-1}-(n+1)\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\binom{n}{k-l-1}\binom{m+1}{l}

\displaystyle=(m-n)\binom{m+n+1}{k-1}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle(L_{m}L_{n}-L_{n}L_{m})(t^{p}\otimes{w}_{1})

\displaystyle=(m-n)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\binom{m+n+1}{k}t^{m+n+p+1-k}\otimes L_{k-1}{w}_{1}

\displaystyle=[L_{m},L_{n}](t^{p}\otimes{w}_{1}),

which implies that the action defines a representation of the Virasoro algebra of central charge
0
.
The latter property regarding the degree is obvious from the definition.
∎

Now we define the bilinear functional
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\colon W_{\infty}(0)\times(\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0})\to\mathbb{C}
by

(B.3)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes y\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right):=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\sigma(y)(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}})

for
n\in\mathbb{Z}
,
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
and
y\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{<0})
, where
\sigma
is the anti-involution of
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir})
defined by
\sigma(X):=-X
,
X\in\mathfrak{vir}
.

Step 5

Finally we extend the bilinear functional to the whole space
W_{\infty}\times(\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0})
.
For that purpose, we define an action of the Virasoro algebra on
\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0}
by the so-called coproduct action:

X((t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0}):=X(t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0}+(t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes X{w}_{0},\quad X\in\mathfrak{vir}.

Then
\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0}

becomes a representation of the Virasoro algebra of central charge
c
.

Note that the tensor product
\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0}
is naturally
\mathbb{Z}
-graded so that

\displaystyle\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0}\right)(n),

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0}\right)(n)

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{k,r\in\mathbb{N}}\left(\mathbb{C}t^{k+r-n-1}\otimes W_{1}(k)\right)\otimes W_{0}(r).

We extend
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}
to a bilinear functional
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\colon W_{\infty}\times(\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0})\to\mathbb{C}
by

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(y\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},w):=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\theta(y)w),\quad y\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{<0}),\quad w\in\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0},

where
\theta
is the anti-involution of
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir})
defined by
\theta(L_{n})=L_{-n}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\theta(C)=C
.

Now we define the linear map (
B.1
) that is meant to be an intertwining operator of the desired type.

Definition B.4
.

Define a linear map

\mathcal{Y}\colon W_{1}\to\mathrm{Hom}(W_{0},W_{\infty}^{\prime})\{\bm{x}\}

by the matrix elements

\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}:=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{\mathbb{M}}({w}_{\infty},(t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0})\bm{x}^{\Delta-m-1},

where
{w}_{0}\in W_{0}
,
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
and
{w}_{\infty}\in W_{\infty}
are arbitrary, and
\Delta=h_{\infty}-h_{1}-h_{0}
.

B.2.
Some properties of the linear map

We are going to show that the linear map
\mathcal{Y}
constructed in Definition
B.4
gives an intertwining operator.
We begin with collecting some immediate properties.

Lemma B.5
.

Let
w\in\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0}
. Then we have:

(1)

If
w\in(\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0})(k)
and
{w}_{\infty}\in W_{\infty}(l)
, we have
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left({w}_{\infty},w\right)=0
unless
k=l
.

(2)

For any
y\in\mathfrak{vir}_{<0}
, we have
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},yw\right)=0
.

(3)

For any
y\in\mathfrak{vir}_{0}
, we have
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},yw\right)=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left(y\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},w\right)
.

Proof.

(1): Writing
{w}_{\infty}=y\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}}
with
y\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{<0})
,
we have
\theta(y)w\in(\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0})(k-l)
.
Hence it suffices to show that

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},w)=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},w)=0,\quad w\in(\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0})(k)

unless
k\neq 0
.
We may further assume that

w=(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes y^{\prime}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}
with

y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{<0})
to find

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},w)=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\sigma(y^{\prime})(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}).

Since by assumption

\deg(\sigma(y^{\prime})(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1}))=k
, this value vanishes unless
k=0

from the definition (
B.2
)
of
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}
.

(2): It is sufficient to consider the case

y=L_{-n}
,
n>0
, and
w=(t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes y_{2}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}
,
m\in\mathbb{Z}
,
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
,
y_{2}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{<0})
. Then, since
L_{-n}y_{2}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{<0})
, we observe that

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{-n}\left((t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes y_{2}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{-n}(t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes y_{2}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)+\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},(t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes L_{-n}y_{2}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\sigma(y_{2})L_{-n}(t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)+\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\sigma(L_{-n}y_{2})(t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\sigma(y_{2})L_{-n}(t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\sigma(y_{2})L_{-n}(t^{m}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle 0.

(3): The assertion is obvious for
y=C
,
so it suffices to consider the case of
y=L_{0}
.
First, we assume that
w=(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\in\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0}(0)
, where
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}(k)
is homogeneous. In this case,

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{0}\left((t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\delta_{k-n-1,0}\Bigl(\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{-1}{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)+\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{0}{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},{w}_{1}\otimes L_{0}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\delta_{k-n-1,0}\left((-h_{1}-k-h_{0}+h_{\infty})+h_{1}+k+h_{0}\right)\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}\left(L_{0}\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right).

Then consider a general vector
{w}_{0}\in W_{0}
, and write it as

{w}_{0}=y^{\prime}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}
for
y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{<0})
.
For
n\in\mathbb{Z}
and
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
,
we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{0}\left((t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes y^{\prime}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{0}\left(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1}\right)\otimes y^{\prime}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)+\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes L_{0}y^{\prime}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right).

Note that
[L_{0},y^{\prime}]\in U(\mathfrak{vir}_{<0})
and
\sigma([L_{0},y^{\prime}])=-[\sigma(y^{\prime}),L_{0}]
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{0}\left((t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes y^{\prime}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\sigma(y^{\prime})L_{0}\left(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1}\right)\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)+\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\sigma(y^{\prime})\left(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1}\right)\otimes L_{0}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},[\sigma(y^{\prime}),L_{0}](t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L_{0}\left(\sigma(y^{\prime})(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty,0)}\left(L_{0}\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\sigma(y^{\prime})(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}^{(\infty)}\left(L_{0}\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes y^{\prime}\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right).

This finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition B.6
.

The bilinear functional
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}
is
\theta
-invariant in the sense that

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(y{w}_{\infty},w)=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}({w}_{\infty},\theta(y)w),\quad y\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}),\quad{w}_{\infty}\in W_{\infty},\quad w\in\widehat{W}_{1}\otimes W_{0}.

Proof.

Let us write
{w}_{\infty}=y_{+}\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}}
, where
y_{+}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{<0})
.
Since it follows from the PBW theorem that
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir})=(\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{<0})\,\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{0}))\oplus\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir})\mathfrak{vir}_{>0}
,
we can uniquely write
yy_{+}=y_{1}+y_{2}
,
y_{1}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{<0})\,\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{0})
,
y_{2}\in\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir})\mathfrak{vir}_{>0}
.
Since
y_{2}
annihilates the highest weight vector,

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(y{w}_{\infty},w)=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(yy_{+}\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},w)=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(y_{1}\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},w).

From the definition of
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}
and (3) of Lemma
B.5
(note that
\theta|_{\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{0})}=\mathrm{id}_{\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir}_{0})}
), we have

\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(y{w}_{\infty},w)=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\theta(y_{1})w).

Notice also that
\theta(y_{2})\in\mathfrak{vir}_{<0}\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{vir})
. Then, (2) of Lemma
B.5
gives
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\theta(y_{2})w)=0
.
Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(y{w}_{\infty},w)

\displaystyle\;=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\theta(y_{1}+y_{2})w)=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\theta(yy_{+})w)=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\theta(y_{+})\theta(y)w)

\displaystyle\;=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}(y_{+}\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\theta(y)w)=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}({w}_{\infty},\theta(y)w)

as desired.
∎

B.3.
Translation property

We show that the linear map
\mathcal{Y}
satisfies the Jacobi identity (
4.18
) and the translation property (
4.19
).
Let us begin with the easier one; the translation property.

Proposition B.7
.

For
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
, we have

\mathcal{Y}(L_{-1}{w}_{1},\bm{x})=\frac{d}{d\bm{x}}\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x}).

Proof.

We take arbitrary vectors
{w}_{0}\in W_{0}(k)
,
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}(l)
and
{w}_{\infty}\in W_{\infty}(m)

and consider the matrix element

\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}(L_{-1}{w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\widehat{\mathbb{M}}({w}_{\infty},(t^{n}\otimes L_{-1}{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0})\bm{x}^{\Delta-n-1}.

Due to (1) of Lemma
B.5
, the coefficient
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}({w}_{\infty},(t^{n}\otimes L_{-1}{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0})

vanishes unless
n=k+l-m
.
Hence, we have

\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}(L_{-1}{w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}({w}_{\infty},(t^{k+l-m}\otimes L_{-1}{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0})\bm{x}^{\Delta-k-l+m-1}.

Recall that the action of
L_{0}
on
(t^{n-1}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0}
,
n\in\mathbb{Z}
reads

\displaystyle L_{0}\left((t^{n-1}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0}\right)=(t^{n}\otimes L_{-1}{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0}+(h_{1}+k+h_{0}+l)(t^{n-1}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0}.

Rearranging the terms, we get

\displaystyle\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}(L_{-1}{w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\bm{x}^{\Delta-k-l+m-1}\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left({w}_{\infty},L_{0}\left((t^{k+l-m-1}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\bm{x}^{\Delta-k-l+m-1}(h_{1}+k+h_{0}+l)\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left({w}_{\infty},(t^{k+l-m-1}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0}\right).

Employing the
\theta
-invariance (Proposition
B.6
) of the bilinear functional
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}(L_{-1}{w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\Delta-k-l+m)\bm{x}^{\Delta-k-l+m-1}\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left({w}_{\infty},(t^{k+l-m-1}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\bm{x}}\left(x^{\Delta-k-l+m}\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left({w}_{\infty},(t^{k+l-m-1}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0}\right)\right).

Here, we again observe the identity

\displaystyle\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}=x^{\Delta-k-l+m}\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left({w}_{\infty},(t^{k+l-m-1}\otimes{w}_{1})\otimes{w}_{0}\right)

to conclude the desired result.
∎

B.4.
Jacobi identity

We next show the Jacobi identity (
4.18
).
Since the Virasoro VOA is generated by the conformal vector
\omega
, it suffices to show the Jacobi identity for
v=\omega
.
For convenience, we introduce the generating series
L(\bm{\zeta})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}L_{n}\bm{\zeta}^{-n-2}
whose coefficients are understood as the action of the Virasoro algebra on any representation.
The following commutation relations are sometimes useful:

(B.4)

\displaystyle[L_{n},L(\bm{\zeta})]

\displaystyle=\bm{\zeta}^{n+1}\frac{d}{d\bm{\zeta}}L(\bm{\zeta})+2(n+1)\bm{\zeta}^{n}L(\bm{\zeta})+\frac{c}{2}\binom{n+1}{3}\bm{\zeta}^{n-2},\quad n\in\mathbb{Z},

where
c
is the central charge of representations under consideration.

In our setting, it is convenient to formulate the Jacobi
identity in terms of matrix elements. We will therefore prove the following.

Proposition B.8
.

For
{w}_{0}\in W_{0}
,
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
and
{w}_{\infty}\in W_{\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{\zeta}_{0}^{-1}\delta\left(\frac{\bm{\zeta}_{1}-\bm{x}}{\bm{\zeta}_{0}}\right)\braket{{w}_{\infty},L(\bm{\zeta}_{1})\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}-\bm{\zeta}_{0}^{-1}\delta\left(\frac{\bm{x}-\bm{\zeta}_{1}}{-\bm{\zeta}_{0}}\right)\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x})L(\bm{\zeta}_{1}){w}_{0}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\bm{x}^{-1}\delta\left(\frac{\bm{\zeta}_{1}-\bm{\zeta}_{0}}{\bm{x}}\right)\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}\left(L(\bm{\zeta}_{0}){w}_{1},\bm{x}\right){w}_{0}}

In the following proof, it will be convenient to use the notation

\mathcal{Y}_{t}({w}_{1},\bm{x}):=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}(t^{n}\otimes{w}_{1})\bm{x}^{\Delta-n-1}\in\widehat{W}_{1}\{\bm{x}\}

for
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
.
Then applying the bilinear functional
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}
coefficientwise to
formal series in
\bm{x}
, we may understand

\Braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}({w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}_{t}({w}_{1},\bm{x})\otimes{w}_{0})

for
{w}_{0}\in W_{0}
,
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
and
{w}_{\infty}\in W_{\infty}
.
The following formula derived from Proposition
B.3
will be also useful:

(B.5)

L_{n}\mathcal{Y}_{t}({w}_{1},\bm{x})=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\binom{n+1}{k}\bm{x}^{n+1-k}\mathcal{Y}_{t}(L_{k-1}{w}_{1},\bm{x}),\quad n\in\mathbb{Z}.

The strategy of proving Proposition
B.8
is to reduce the Jacobi identity to the
commutativity
and the
associativity
.
Let us first observe the commutativity.

Lemma B.9
.

For any
{w}_{0}\in W_{0}
,
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
and
{w}_{\infty}\in W_{\infty}
, we have

\displaystyle\braket{{w}_{\infty},L(\bm{\zeta}_{1})\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}-\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x})L(\bm{\zeta}_{1}){w}_{0}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}_{\bm{\zeta}_{0}}\bm{x}^{-1}\delta\left(\frac{\bm{\zeta}_{1}-\bm{\zeta}_{0}}{\bm{x}}\right)\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}\left(L(\bm{\zeta}_{0}){w}_{1},\bm{x}\right){w}_{0}}.

Proof.

From the property of the contragredient module (Lemma
4.3
)
and the
\theta
-invariance of the bilinear functional
\widehat{\mathbb{M}}
(Proposition
B.6
),
we can see that

\braket{{w}_{\infty},L(\bm{\zeta}_{1})\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}({w}_{\infty},L(\bm{\zeta}_{1})(\mathcal{Y}_{t}({w}_{1},\bm{x})\otimes{w}_{0})).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\braket{{w}_{\infty},L(\bm{\zeta}_{1})\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}-\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x})L(\bm{\zeta}_{1}){w}_{0}}=\widehat{\mathbb{M}}({w}_{\infty},L(\bm{\zeta}_{1})\mathcal{Y}_{t}({w}_{1},\bm{x})\otimes{w}_{0}).

Therefore, the proof amounts to showing the identity

\displaystyle L(\bm{\zeta}_{1})\mathcal{Y}_{t}({w}_{1},\bm{x})=\mathrm{Res}_{\bm{\zeta}_{0}}\bm{x}^{-1}\delta\left(\frac{\bm{\zeta}_{1}-\bm{\zeta}_{0}}{\bm{x}}\right)\mathcal{Y}_{t}\left(L(\bm{\zeta}_{0}){w}_{1},\bm{x}\right)

in
\widehat{W}_{1}\{\bm{x}\}
, which is straightforward from (
B.5
).
∎

We next state the associativity.

Proposition B.10
.

For any
{w}_{0}\in W_{0}
, there exists
k\in\mathbb{N}

(depending on
{w}_{0}
) such that

\displaystyle(\bm{\zeta}_{0}+\bm{x})^{k}\braket{{w}_{\infty},L(\bm{\zeta}_{0}+\bm{x})\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}=(\bm{\zeta}_{0}+\bm{x})^{k}\braket{{w}_{\infty},\mathcal{Y}(L(\bm{\zeta}_{0}){w}_{1},\bm{x}){w}_{0}}

holds for any
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
and
{w}_{\infty}\in W_{\infty}
.

The proof of Proposition
B.10
requires some preliminaries.

Lemma B.11
.

Let
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}_{\bm{\zeta}_{0}}\bm{\zeta}_{0}^{-1}(\bm{\zeta}_{0}+\bm{x})^{2}\braket{\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L(\bm{\zeta}_{0}+\bm{x})\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x})\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}_{\bm{\zeta}_{0}}\bm{\zeta}_{0}^{-1}(\bm{\zeta}_{0}+\bm{x})^{2}\braket{\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\mathcal{Y}\left(L(\bm{\zeta}_{0}){w}_{1},\bm{x}\right)\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}}.

Proof.

Assuming that
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}(k)
is homogeneous,
we can see that both sides coincide with

h_{\infty}\bm{x}^{\Delta-k}\mathbb{M}^{(\infty,0)}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},{w}_{1}\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right).

∎

Lemma B.12
.

For any
{w}_{1}\in W_{1}
and
i\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle(\bm{\zeta}_{0}+\bm{x})^{2+i}\braket{\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L(\bm{\zeta}_{0}+\bm{x})\mathcal{Y}({w}_{1},\bm{x})\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle(\bm{\zeta}_{0}+\bm{x})^{2+i}\braket{\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},\mathcal{Y}\left(L(\bm{\zeta}_{0}){w}_{1},\bm{x}\right)\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}}.

Proof.

Note that the assertion is equivalent to that

(B.6)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Res}_{\bm{\zeta}_{0}}\bm{\zeta}_{0}^{m}(\bm{\zeta}_{0}+\bm{x})^{2+i}\widehat{\mathbb{M}}\left(\bar{w}_{c,h_{\infty}},L(\bm{\zeta}_{0}+\bm{x})\left(\mathcal{Y}_{t}({w}_{1},\bm{x})\otimes\bar{w}_{c,h_{0}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\;
